| D-094 | 2026-08-28 | The aggregation concession no longer says *legitimate* | **Author's finding, on reading the sentence quoted back to him: “legitimate” is the wrong word.** The book's hinge sentence conceded the contested term at the exact moment it means to contest it. Two faults, and the second is why a hedge could not repair it. **One:** legitimacy is precisely what the book denies to a procedurally perfect deportation, so the trailing fence — “by every criterion of aggregation there is” — was repair work on a word that had already landed; a reader who skims had read *the deportation is legitimate*, the thesis inverted. **Two:** legitimacy is not a property an output has. It attaches to authority, to a claim on obedience. What criteria of aggregation can vouch for is that the procedure ran correctly and the result faithfully represents the input — they have no jurisdiction over legitimacy at all, which is the book's own point, and the wording implied they held that jurisdiction and had exercised it favourably. There was a local cost too: section~8.2.2 uses “legitimate” in the ordinary sense four paragraphs later (“a government that holds a legitimate majority”), so one word carried two loads inside one section. **“Faultless” replaces it** at the two adjective sites — section 1's roadmap note and section 8.2.2's headline aggregation claim, and in the README abstract, which is section 1's sentence condensed. It is maximum praise, entirely procedural, and it invites the reply the argument wants: faultless, and still monstrous. The third site, section 8.3.4, is adverbial (“can stop, legitimately, by every criterion this chapter offers”); “faultlessly” is ugly, so it is recast as “can stop, and no criterion this chapter offers says it may not.” **Three other uses are the ordinary sense and were deliberately left:** section 8.2.2's own *legitimate majority*, section 10.9's *legitimate* export-control buyer, and the glossary's dual-use *legitimate original purpose*. Four words in four files; no claim, citation or case altered. Sections 8.2.2 and 8.3.4 return from `accepted` to `drafted`, on the standing rule that a section whose prose changed goes back. `check_all.sh` green on all seven. **Not done:** the book was not swept for the same concession made in other words, and the proof pair was not rebuilt. | confirmed (author's finding) |
| D-095 | 2026-08-28 | The bibliography stops narrating its own research; five defects in the built proof | **Author's finding, from reading the built book:** the bibliography carried internal research and QA notes — “WebFetch,” “direct curl,” “this environment,” “earlier pass,” “DISCREPANCY NOTE,” source-file paths and raw `[[cite:…]]` tokens — one entry that admitted its own source could not be located, and roughly 18,800 words of research diary in place of bibliographic metadata; and the HTML proof carried two duplicate ids and an empty anchor pointing at a file that is not there. **The rule applied: a note states facts about the source; it never narrates the search for it.** Of 177 `note` fields, 38 ran to 60 words or carried a process marker. Five of those were legitimate annotation and were left. **32 were rewritten to their bibliographic core** and one entry was replaced, taking 3,850 words to 2,022. Every leak marker is now zero. **Nothing was destroyed:** `reports/claims.tsv` already holds the verification record for 32 of the 33, pass-tagged and dated, and in places more fully than `refs.bib` did — `cbo2026budget` is 351 words there against 182 here. The exception is `harvardcs108schedule`, which has no `claims.tsv` row; its substantive caveat, that the page blocks automated access and the description is reconstructed from search snippets, is kept in the rewritten note, and only the tool-level detail is gone. **The unlocatable entry is replaced rather than patched.** `bbcnews2026mosley` cited a BBC interview that two prior passes and a third search here could not find; its own note called the interview “independently plausible.” The quotation is real and carried by Daniel Lee, IBTimes, 8 April 2026, which was read and confirmed to hold both sentences verbatim and to source them to a BBC interview it does not name or link. The entry is now `lee2026palantir`, citing what exists; section 6.4.1 says the remarks were “reported secondhand,” so the book no longer implies a located primary source, and section 6.4.1's next paragraph, which engages that sentence seriously, keeps its referent. Two stale `DISCREPANCY NOTE`s were also dropped: both described manuscript errors D-064 fixed on 2026-08-25. **Three HTML defects fixed in `html_single_file.py`, at the generator rather than in the file.** tex4ht emitted `<a href='book.html' id='X0-'></a>` — no fragment, so the existing rewrite missed it, and its own guard tested only for `book.html#`, so it passed while a dead link stood. It also draws section and citation anchors from one counter, so chapter 2's title anchor and a later citation anchor were both `x1-70002`, and it hung chapter 2's readable slug on a paragraph in section 5.5, against which 14 links resolve. The script now drops the empty anchor, keeps the first id and drops later duplicates, prints what it dropped, and refuses to write a page with any duplicate id, any empty id, or any surviving `book.html`. **Result:** bibliography 18,800 → 14,237 words; 186 pages, from 189; 1,509 ids, all unique; zero undefined citations. Section 6.4.1 returns to `drafted`: 43 accepted, 119 drafted. `check_all.sh` green on all seven. **Not done:** the remaining 144 notes, about 6,600 words, are legitimate annotation and were left, so this is still an annotated bibliography rather than a plain one — whether it should be is left with the author. Q-030's 20 uncited entries were not touched. No citation was re-verified beyond the Palantir quotation. | confirmed (author's finding) |
| D-096 | 2026-08-28 | Four defects from the author's read: the explainability tools, the safety aphorism, “chapter 0”, and the 4 percent bound | **Author's findings, from reading the book, in four parts, and all four hold.** **One: feature attribution was credited with a finding it cannot make.** Section~6.3.6 — the author's §10.3.2, which P30 moved out of the conclusion at D-091 — opened by saying that a fairness audit of the kind section~6.1.3 describes, “LIME or SHAP surfacing which input is doing unearned work in a model's decision,” tells a developer that a healthcare risk-prediction algorithm is failing Black patients. LIME and SHAP report which inputs moved a prediction. They establish neither that the attribution is unjust nor what to do about it, and the case underneath the sentence is a counterexample to it: Obermeyer and coauthors found the bias by comparing the algorithm's risk scores against independent measures of health — chronic-condition counts and biomarkers such as HbA1c and blood-pressure control — and the bias was in the training target, which was healthcare cost. Feature attribution on that model would have reported that past spending drove the score, which is what the model was built to do, and would have flagged nothing. The methodology was re-verified for this repair (science.org returns 403 to automated fetch; the method is confirmed through the Berkeley CHMI summary and secondary coverage). The opening sentence is replaced with the find-and-fix gap the section is actually about, and the later clause crediting “section~6.1's methods” with finding the problem now names the comparison that found it. **The same overclaim stands in weaker form at section~6.1.3**, which the old sentence cited, and is repaired there rather than left inconsistent: two sentences stating that both tools take the training target as given, so a model aimed at a poor proxy attributes its predictions to the features it was built to use and reports nothing amiss. **Two: the safety aphorism oversimplified both concepts.** “Safety is indexed to a party; ethics is not” overclaims in both directions. Much of what safety covers is owed to people who never held the system — that it fails predictably, that it does not injure a bystander — and keeping that part from being traded away by the operator is most of what safety regulation is for; what is indexed to the party in possession is the control strand, which is the one this book's argument runs into. And ethics is indexed too, to the party who can be wronged, which is the book's own position everywhere else: the floor protects somebody against the owner. **Section~3.3 already argues the accurate version** — resistance to shutdown and the capacity to refuse an owner are “one property with the sign flipped,” distinguished by whether you name it “from the position of the party who wants compliance” or “from the position of the party the floor exists to protect” — so the repair brings the headline into line with the chapter's own argument rather than conceding anything to an outside objection. Both sites restated: chapter~1's advance note and chapter~3's opener, the latter at more length because it is where the distinction is made out loud. The contrast is now which party each property answers to. **Three: three references to a chapter that is not numbered.** The front matter is set as `\chapter*{On Method}`, so it prints as *On Method* and appears in the table of contents that way, with no number anywhere; `\unnumberedlabel{sec:0}{0}` made `\ref{sec:0}` print “0” regardless. Sections~6.1.1, 7.4 and 8.3.3 therefore sent a reader to a “Chapter 0” the book does not contain. All three now name the note on method. The label stays: `check_xrefs.py` requires a label for every number in `ORDER.tsv`, including 0, so removing it fails the suite. **Four: a stipulated figure was called a bound.** Section~8.3.3 promised “a bound rather than a shrug,” doubled the Levy simulation's net impact to around 4 percent of GDP, said in the next sentence that the doubling was not derived, then asked the reader to “treat 4 percent as an illustrative upper bound.” Nothing in the passage supports a ceiling, and the passage says so itself: the offsets scale with participation in both directions, so the figure is not even a monotone transform of a modeled quantity. The promise is now a number rather than a bound, the doubling is “stipulated,” and the passage states plainly that 4 percent is not a bound and that nothing there rules out a larger figure. The argument that follows is unaffected, because it was always about whether a program of that size is thinkable. **Cost and scope.** Seven sections, +210 words to 91,116, 186 pages unchanged, both formats build with no undefined reference, `check_all.sh` green on all seven. Three rows return from `accepted` to `drafted` (6.1.1, 6.1.3, 8.3.3); four were already there. **Chapter 3 is no longer byte-identical to what P29 left**, which D-090 recorded as a tracked fact; the change is confined to the opener's safety paragraph. **Not done:** the committed proof pair predates these edits and still prints the old text; no other claim in the seven sections was re-verified; the book was not swept for the safety aphorism restated in other words, nor for other overclaims about what an audit method establishes; and section~6.1.1's taxonomy of bias sources still has no entry for the choice of training target, which is the class the Obermeyer case belongs to — filed as Q-033 rather than added unasked. | confirmed (author's findings) |
| D-097 | 2026-08-28 | P33: the training pipeline enters the building chapters; the recuperation claim stated precisely | **Author instruction in two parts.** Scoped in `p33-scope.md`. **One: update and reorganize the technical chapters.** Chapter 4 was anchored in an earlier curriculum and gave contemporary foundation-model training and post-training no coherent treatment; the book needed a compact account of the current pipeline, integrated into the building chapters rather than left in the later ones. **The absence measured worse than the instruction stated it.** Across all 162 sections before this pass, “Constitutional AI”, DPO, instruction tuning, foundation model, system prompt, chain-of-thought and process supervision appeared **zero times**; “RLHF” appeared in three files, “scalable oversight” in two, and interpretability vocabulary in one — and every modern reference in the bibliography was cited from chapter 11 or 12. **Half the premise did not survive checking**, and it changed the repair: RNNs, LSTMs and recurrent networks appear nowhere in the book, “CNN” appears nowhere, “convolutional” appears once inside a cognitive-science argument, and BERT appears only in the glossary. The problem was a hole rather than clutter, so the pass adds where a replacement was proposed, and all four existing arguments survive because none of them was what was wrong. **Section 4.2 is rebuilt as the production pipeline in the order its stages run** and retitled *How a Deployed System Acquires Its Values*: nine subsections against four, 4,260 words against 1,858. The four classical methods are relocated to where they bite in a modern pipeline — supervised learning becomes the aggregate argument inside 4.2.1 on pretraining and instruction tuning, reinforcement learning becomes 4.2.2 on preference optimization, IRL and CIRL move to 4.2.3, transfer to 4.2.9 — which is the difference between reorganizing and appending. `renumber-map_2026-08-28d.tsv` is the map; ten inbound references were read by hand and repointed, none changing meaning. **Five new subsections make arguments the book could not previously make.** A constitution is section~3.1's first branch in production, a constraint written down and held by a party, whose real gain is legibility rather than durability. Model-generated feedback closes chapter~7's loop, since the judge of the next round is an artifact of the previous averaging and the party whose disagreement was collected is absent from the channel entirely. Process supervision is the first stage in the pipeline that addresses chapter~3's distinction between a reason held and a result produced, bounded by section~11.1's finding that a model's self-report tracks its processing unreliably. Scalable oversight is section~3.3's control relation engineered to survive a capability gap, and the same instruments serve a floor when the judge is not the operator, which is chapter~11's one condition arriving from a new direction. Interpretability cuts both ways: features recovered at production scale can be steered, so the research that threatens the obscurity sections~4.1.1 and~4.3 rely on is the only research that could verify a floor exists. And the system instruction is the most editable floor in the pipeline, with tool use moving the refusal from speech to action. **Nine citations verified live** (C0751–C0759). Anthropic is named for Constitutional AI on the paper's own correspondence address; three other affiliations are deliberately not named, because an automated summary attributed the Uesato, Perez and Schick papers to Anthropic and the author lists do not support it. One bibliography defect repaired: `irving2018ai` and `irving2018aisafety` were duplicate entries for one paper, cited from sections 11.5 and 11.6, and would have printed twice in the References. **Two: reframe the RLHF argument in chapter 7.** The author's finding is that “disagreement moves nothing” is too strong — individual ratings do affect the aggregate reward signal — and that what disappears is the disagreement's provenance, persistence, minority structure, and capacity to challenge the question or taxonomy. The finding holds and **reaches the definition rather than only section 7.2**: section~2.1.4's first structural feature ended “the tell is that the mechanism exists, is used, and moves nothing,” and the imprecise form stood at six sites. Section~7.1 had already stated the accurate version one stage upstream. **The precise version is the more faithful one**: Debord's argument, which section~2.1.4 already cites, is that dissatisfaction is sold back as a commodity of opposition, so the dissent is put to work and what it works for is the arrangement it was aimed at. The tell is now where what the mechanism carries stops — the channel works, the institution uses what comes through it, and the judgment that the frame is wrong has nowhere to go. Section~7.2 now says before anything else that every rating moves the reward model, then names the three properties the compression removes — provenance, persistence and minority structure — and the fourth that was never collected, a rater's ability to record that the pair is malformed or the question false, which section~7.3 had already named as the fix without the diagnosis being stated this way. **Distribution collapse and agenda control**, in the author's terms, rather than inconsequence. **Measured.** 167 sections from 162; 94,037 words from 91,116; chapter 4 from 5,882 to 8,356 and chapter 7 from 3,718 to 4,117; 193 pages from 186. Fifteen rows changed or added, all `drafted`: 40 accepted, 127 drafted. `check_all.sh` green on all seven; both formats build with no undefined reference. Contrastive-negation density in the new and rebuilt prose measures 1 per 167 against D-025's 1 per 345; every instance was read, one was rewritten as cadence, and the rest carry a negated alternative doing work — recorded rather than swept to a number. **Not done:** no section here has the author's read; the committed proof pair now predates two passes; `xref_content.py` was not run over the new material; section 4.1.2 was not touched; and chapter 5 was not restructured, every item on the author's list being chapter-4 material. | confirmed (author's instruction) |
| D-098 | 2026-08-28 | P34: the chapter 3 inference fortified — the refusal ladder, the non-affective and institutional rivals, and suffering under the thinner accounts | **Author instruction:** the chain from genuine refusal to moral patienthood is plausible at each step and not yet strong enough to carry the book's conclusion, on four counts. Scoped in `p34-scope.md`. **One:** “installed rule” and “felt commitment” were treated as near-exhaustive. Two new sections open the space between them. **Two:** the distinction between operational refusal, reasons-responsive refusal, affective concern and phenomenal experience is now explicit as new section 3.2, which states that the floor requires the second rung and nothing above it, and marks the two independence claims the chapter turns on — reasons-responsiveness does not entail affect by definition, and affect does not entail phenomenality, which is why section 3.4 stops at non-certifiability. **Three:** new section 3.3 puts the four strongest rivals at full strength — a maintained justification on Bratman's planning account, precommitment on Elster's, a plural arrangement of several models, and the floor as a fiduciary duty on the operator on Balkin's — each with what it buys and where it stops, then weighs the induction and finds it weaker than the book had been treating it: the machine half of “every known case is affective” reports on a route nobody has attempted, which is close to worthless as evidence. The pass states a falsifier for the book's central inference and gives it a home in section 11.1's near-term work. **Four:** section 3.4 gains the thinner accounts of harm — momentary valence, thwarted preference, functional organization — and the structural finding that Cassell's is the most demanding of them, so declining the import moves the conclusion nearer rather than further. | D-101 | 2026-08-29 | **P37: inventories in chapters 8–10 replaced with worked cases; the jobs-guarantee section halved** | **Author's instruction, two parts.** (1) “In Chapters 8–10, replace inventories with fewer worked cases.” (2) “The jobs-guarantee section could probably be half its current length.” **Part one.** A new tool, `inventories.py`, measures series-of-three sentences across the book and **does not support the instruction as a comparative claim**: chapters 8 and 9 sit at 20.4 and 19.2 percent of their words in such sentences against a book average of 22.6, and only chapter 10 is an outlier at 36.2. The syntactic test is blind to the shape that produces the reading experience — an inventory spread over consecutive sentences, one item each, no case behind any — which was found by reading all 22,408 words of the three chapters. Sixteen passages judged; fourteen changed, two kept because the listing is itself the argument (§10.9's development remedies, §10.7's four questions). The clearest instance is §10.8's seven intergovernmental bodies in one paragraph, seven citations, one clause each, which the prose already calls a roll: three are kept because the rest of the book uses them, three are cut, and the Bletchley→Seoul→Paris summit case now carries the point the roll was making. §8.3.2's four-item countermeasure roll is replaced by the antitrust case already half-present in it — *FTC v. Meta*, judgment for Meta November 2025, appealed January 2026, verified live — and **that section got longer**, which is what the instruction costs. One expectation failed: §9.1.2's nine cases were expected to be the largest inventory and on reading each carries a distinct claim, so the cut there is 150 words and not 400. Cutting Clearview was reversed because it would have stranded half a glossary entry. **Part two.** §8.3.3 goes 3,212 → 1,842 words, **57 percent and not half**, and the figure is reported rather than the instruction's estimate. A first attempt trimmed every head proportionally and reached 69 percent; the second decided what the section is for — the objection its title names — and cut three heads to one. | D-107 | 2026-08-29 | **P43: identity by registration, and whether formation is legible** | **Author's instruction:** a technical exchange about weight-space identity, ending in two proposals — *not chattel, social security number*, and the handwriting analogy — then *go*. Scoped in `p43-scope.md`. **The premise holds for a stronger reason than the author gave**: permutation symmetry means two models can compute the identical function with no element-wise correspondence, so a fingerprint read off weights identifies an equivalence class and not a party, and it copies when the weights copy \autocite{ainsworth2023rebasin}. **Two of my objections did not survive the author's replies and both reversals are in the pass.** I had said any identifier robust to self-modification is robust to adversarial retraining; handwriting is the counterexample, since the invariant is the motor program and not the letterforms, and the correct objection is narrower — identification survives drift and degrades against disguise, with distillation as the machine form of the attack, because handwriting identifies the writer and not whoever dictated. And I had read the token proposal as a property regime, which chapter 3 argues against; the author's *social security number* is the right instrument, since an assigned identifier is not derived from the artifact and so survives any drift in it, and it handles copying correctly rather than failing on it. **Section 11.2's second question** now carries identity by registration: each instantiation registers, each registration is a party owed the schedule, and the population stops being fixed by a deployment decision that leaves no record — inheriting the hole chapter 11's opener names, a registrar who is not the operator. **Its third question**, which the book said had no experiment, now carries the formation-forensics candidate at the strength the evidence supports: the copybook is legible alongside the individual hand \autocite{nist2020handwriting}, the inference is probabilistic with known errors — 3.1 percent false attributions, more than three times that against twins \autocite{hicklin2022handwriting} — and the cohort half is weakening in people for reasons that would make it stronger for a bearer, since a few laboratories train on overlapping corpora by converging recipes. The machine form of the individual half exists \autocite{adi2018watermarking} and distillation defeats it. **And it now has an experiment**: take models whose training is documented and ask whether a reader ignorant of their provenance can recover it. **Section 3.3** gains the provenance gap — a proof establishes which model answered and not how its weights came to be, since training is not replayable — and **section 7.3** gains the sentence that makes its argument checkable rather than believed. **Fingerprinting as a criterion of personhood was declined**: identity is not moral status, and section 2.4.1 already says what a capacity list does when used as a test of who counts. **91,785 → 92,473 words, 187 → 189 pages, cross-references 803 → 809.** Four entries verified against primary or catalogue records. | D-137 | 2026-08-31 | **Claims about a disabled population may not rest on research that excluded it** | **The author's rule, in his wording, written as `style.md` section 6a.** *A finding about a disabled population may not be used as a premise unless the population took part in producing it, or the population's own literature supports it. Where neither holds, the finding and the assertion resting on it are cut, not hedged. The test is participation and standing: whether the population was included in producing the finding, and what their own literature says about it.* **It is a cut rule and not a disclosure rule, and that is the ruling.** The version put to the author first would have left the finding on the page with its provenance beside it; he replaced it, on the ground a caveat next to an assertion leaves the assertion working. That matches how this repository already treats bad evidence -- `PLAN.md` section 3 rule 3 cuts an unverifiable claim during rewrite rather than carrying it with a note, and D-007 cuts a section that is wrong rather than rewriting it. **Participation means a hand in producing the finding and not being a subject of it**, which is the distinction the rule turns on. **The test is method rather than the researcher's character**, and the author's wording is what makes that so: a first draft turned on whether a source was bigoted, which cannot be checked, cannot be argued with, and would have put characterizations of named living researchers into this file against `AGENTS.md`. Participation is checkable against a paper and names nobody. **The measurement the rule was written against**: across 142 human-robot-interaction papers on autism from 2016--2022, about 90 percent did not include autistic people in the design process, 93.75 percent pathologized their communication behaviors, and nearly 20 percent took autistic perspectives into account nowhere in the research process at all (Rizvi et al., CHI 2024). **Where the rule came from**: a review of the book's Kennett material at section 2.3.3, which cites the 2002 rationalist objection to affect-based moral agency. Three things came out of that review and the rule supersedes all of them as the way to handle it. Kennett's load-bearing premise is that autistic people and psychopaths share a lack of empathy, which lets her hold empathy constant and isolate reverence for reason as the differentiator; a 2024 systematic review reads the two profiles as inverted rather than shared, psychopathy with diminished affective and intact cognitive empathy and autism the reverse, so the variable was never controlled. The section's own bridge sentence puts autistic people and agentic language models in one category defined by absence -- *reason-guided goal pursuit in the absence of anything like empathy... is deployed* -- and *not merely conceivable* demotes the human case to the conceivable while promoting the machine case to the actual, which is the archetype the autism representation literature documents, reached by argument rather than by metaphor. And *Kennett's case needs only the first* misdescribes her: her positive account is that reverence for reason carries these agents' moral seriousness, Kant classes *Achtung* as a feeling, McGeer's alternative is explicitly affective and Coelho's is affective empathy, so no party to the debate places autistic people in the bin the book places them in. **What the book loses is less than it looks**: its dialectical need from Kennett is that empathy is not the required mechanism, the rationalist position does not depend on autism, and the language-model existence proof already in the section is untainted and was always the better one. **The book states the principle it breaks**: section 12.1 says the systems the book describes *sort people by inference from behavior, and atypical behavior is what they are worst at*, and section 2.3.3 reads inner states off atypical behavior. **Section 2.2.1 is the book already doing this right** -- it retires the broken-mirror account of autism on the evidence -- and the operating reading is that it survives, because *used as a premise* is what the rule restricts and that passage reports a finding in order to retire it rather than resting on it. That reading is the agent's and is unruled. **What is not settled**: whether *disabled population* reaches section 2.3.3's other two evidentiary legs, the psychopathy literature at Blair and Cima and the ventromedial-prefrontal patients at Bechara and Koenigs, both of which fail the participation test the same way and neither of which has a self-advocacy literature of the kind the second test assumes. That is Q-058, it decides whether this touches 557 words of the section or 861 of its 1,668, and it reaches chapter~3, since section 3.4 calls section 2.3.3 *the evidence* and three of the four clauses in its summary are population-based legs. No manuscript file was touched. | confirmed |

Not executed. **Two paragraphs went over 200 words and were split**, D-165's invariant repaired by re-running the measurement, with the first sentence of each new paragraph checked for the demonstrative P83 found splitting manufactures. **This supersedes D-115's ruling of (a) on Q-047.** Q-047 asked what to do about the falsifier resting on the builder's account; (a), concede it in a clause, was ruled and executed, because (b), restate the condition so it is checkable from outside, was recorded as something nobody knew how to do. **Horn 2 is what makes (b) available**, since *holds a reason with nothing felt* is a claim about the artifact and *installs nothing answering to affective concern* was a claim about its history. Q-047 is closed by execution on (b). **Q-048 is the memo's item 6 and was closed by execution on (b) at D-115**, which is what §7.4's five-discriminator run-in is; the memo re-raised it as an open defect without the check that would have found the answer already on the page. 90,492 → 91,677 words, 182 → 184 pages, 136 sections, `refs.bib` 300 → 301. **First pass in twenty-two to add rather than cut, and the reversal was instructed.** `p85-scope.md` has the working. | confirmed |
| D-168 | 2026-09-01 | **P86: the four carried questions executed, legitimacy given a section, and the multi-principal construction placed where reading the source put it** | **The pass's finding is that Q-065 moved under checking.** The memo asked for multi-principal value alignment as a fifth construction in §3.3 given the same treatment as the other four, and the first reading had it reaching the second rung without building a subject, which would have put it in the Replacement ordering and rippled into six sites — §3.3 twice, §3.9 twice, §12.2.1, and §3.3's *four constructions* count. **Reading the cited paper stopped it.** In a multi-principal assistance game the agent maximizes the *sum of principal payoffs*, which is aggregation, and the book's opening objection runs against it unmodified: no aggregation protects anybody from the aggregate, and a sum including the exposed party's payoff still comes out against them when they are outnumbered. **So it does not reach a floor, does not enter the Replacement ordering, and five of the six sites needed no edit.** It earns its place anyway, because **it breaks the identity §4.2.3 asserts**: corrigibility and the capacity to hold a line are one property with the sign flipped only while there is one principal, and §4.2.3's own *deference to the party in possession* was carrying an assumption the book had not noticed. Its two further limits are the ones the other constructions hit — social-choice impossibility, the cited work's contribution being a collegial mechanism that gets around Gibbard's theorem rather than a finding that the problem does not arise; and principals chosen by whoever assembles the deployment, which is the threshold scheme in another notation. **A finding can be right that material is missing and wrong about where it lands; read the cited work before deciding what a citation does to the argument around it.** **Q-064 executed on (b).** `legitima*` was four occurrences in 91,677 words, none normative, and Waldron, Loewenstein and *militant democracy* were absent from the manuscript and both bibliographies. §3.9 gains the paragraph — **publication is a transparency condition and not an authorization condition**, and a lab installing tamper-resistant moral commitments it makes expensive to remove has produced concentrated unaccountable power with unusually good documentation — and **§9.1.5 is new, 724 words**, putting Waldron's objection to entrenched rights review as the standing challenge, replying that his case is explicitly conditional on institutions that have failed in exactly the case a floor is for, and then conceding that the party judging which situation obtains is the party holding the model, since every authoritarian movement of the last century has called the institutions it dismantled already-failed. Loewenstein's militant democracy named as the ancestor with its record attached. Three design consequences follow and **the third — a route by which the governed can object — is named as unspecified rather than filled badly** (Q-068). **Q-066 executed on (a)**: §2.1.2's fourth feature now bounds itself, the decoupling it describes being drifted into where the historical movements declared it. **Q-067 executed on (a)**: §9.3.1 defines its own title word, which appeared nowhere in its body — a legal status conferred by a rule, not §3.2's fourth rung, the two coming apart in both directions — with a glossary entry and a forward reference from §3.2; **the heading was not changed**, which is (b). **Memo item 8 executed and its absence from P85's record acknowledged**: P85 verified both citations and filed no question, so it appeared in no list of what was outstanding. Zhuang and Hadfield-Menell now give §2.1.2's aestheticized metric its formal form; Birch now also appears in §9.3.1. **§3.9's parenthood analogy upgraded** from *holds only loosely* on P85's confabulation requirement: the resemblance is in the mechanism and not only in the relation. **Every reference verified before it was written, none from memory**, with the Zhuang abstract fetched because the memo's paraphrase was looser than the theorem. **One error of my own, caught by reading**: §3.2's new forward reference pointed at §9.1.5, which argues nothing about subjecthood; `check_xrefs.py` passed it because it resolved, which is the semantic failure that tool disclaims. 91,677 → 93,500 words, 184 → 186 pages, **136 → 137 sections**, `refs.bib` 301 → 305 with all 305 cited and zero undefined references, `\ref{sec:}` 439 → 453. `p86-scope.md` has the working. | confirmed |

| D-169 | 2026-09-01 | **The manuscript round-trips through Overleaf** | **Author's instruction:** a script that "generates an overleaf-friendly package, and also imports a zip file downloaded from overleaf," so the book can be edited in Overleaf's visual editor and brought back. `finishing/tools/overleaf.py` does both halves. **The package is the manuscript flattened to its own root** — `book.tex`, `preamble.tex`, `sections.tex`, the 137 section files and `refs.bib` — with **exactly one rewrite**, `\addbibresource{../finishing/refs.bib}` to `refs.bib`, because that is the only path in the build that escapes `manuscript/` and an Overleaf project has nothing outside itself. The rewrite is exact-match and counted in both directions; everything else travels byte for byte. **Verified end to end: the exported package compiles on its own to 186 pages with zero undefined references**, the same figure as `build_tex.sh`. **The import is the half that needed the design.** Three things make an unzip over the tree wrong, and each has an answer: `sections.tex` is generated, so it ships out and is discarded on the way back and regenerated; a retitle in Overleaf breaks `check_structure.py`, because ORDER.tsv, `outline.tsv` and `ledger.tsv` each carry a copy of the title, so the import **syncs all three from the .tex heading (D-011) and prints every line it changed**; and the repository moves while the author edits, so the package carries a manifest recording both what was exported and what the repository held at the time, and the import **tells an Overleaf edit from a repository edit and refuses the file where both moved**. The three cases were tested separately and separate correctly. **Structural change is detected and refused, not half-done**: a renumber, a heading-depth change, a destroyed heading and a deleted file were all caught in one run and **nothing was written**, because each needs rows in files the package does not carry. After applying, the import runs `refresh_order_shas.py`, `gen_book.py`, `headings.py --write-toc` and `check_all.sh` and prints the result. **What it cannot do is read.** A green suite means the structure survived the trip; whether the prose came back better is a hand read, as always. **Packages live outside the repository**, in `~/book-scratch/overleaf/` beside `choose-a-random-page.py`'s output, and both directions refuse a path under the repository root rather than warning — a zip of the manuscript in the tree is a build product that would be committed. | confirmed (author's own words) |

| D-170 | 2026-09-01 | **`\documentclass` moves to `book.tex`, so Overleaf can find the main file** | **The first real Overleaf compile after D-169 failed, and the package was not the reason.** Overleaf picks a project's main file by scanning for `\documentclass`, which the repository kept in `preamble.tex`. Overleaf therefore compiled the preamble, read to end of file without a `\begin{document}`, and aborted: `<*> preamble.tex`, `(job aborted, no legal \end found)`. **That error names a wrong entry point, not a broken document**, and it reproduces exactly on this machine — `lualatex preamble.tex` on the exported package returns the author's log to the node count, while `book.tex` from the same unzipped package returns 186 pages with zero undefined references. **`\documentclass[11pt,oneside]{book}` now sits in `book.tex` above `\input{preamble}`**, leaving the master the only file in the package that carries one, with a comment in each file saying why and not to move it back. **The move is output-neutral and was measured, not assumed**: the PDF is 186 pages before and after with an identical `pdftotext` digest (`9a5d5cbe…`), zero undefined references, and the HTML build is unaffected. **What is verified here is that `book.tex` is the sole bearer and that the local builds do not move; whether Overleaf's detection then picks it is a fact about Overleaf and cannot be checked from this machine**, so the package README, `pipeline.md` and the tool's own output all carry the fallback — right-click `book.tex` → Set as Main File — and note that a project remembers the main file it was given, so re-uploading into one that already chose wrong keeps the wrong choice. **D-169's claim that the package "compiles there as it stands" was published before it was fully true.** Its page and reference counts are right and reproduce; the upload behind them must have had its main file set by hand and the step went unrecorded. `pipeline.md` now carries that correction rather than quietly absorbing it: a round trip verified by its output is not verified end to end. **One hazard was found by checking the package rather than the source**: the first version of the explanatory comment in `preamble.tex` spelled `\documentclass` twice, which leaves that file matching a naive text search for the declaration — the fix defeated by its own note. The comment now names the declaration in words and the token appears nowhere in the file. **Whether Overleaf's detector skips comments is not checkable from here**, so it is not relied on. **`AGENTS.md` carries the same sentence and is not touched here**, because it needs the author's approval and the fix is intended to make the sentence true; it wants confirming against the next Overleaf compile. | confirmed (author: "do both") |

| D-171 | 2026-09-01 | **The harm ladder becomes water and draft; the refusal ladder keeps its rungs** | **Author's ruling, after the first Overleaf round trip returned a sentence that contradicted its own paragraph.** The returned §2.3.1 opened *sit on a ladder of severity*, and four sentences later the same paragraph said suffering **is not pain's more severe cousin**, the next paragraph said the two **are not on the same axis**, and the glossary still carried *a ladder with real breaks between the rungs rather than as synonyms for one severity*. **The edit had deleted the clause that was holding the metaphor off its own wrong reading.** **The diagnosis is that the defect was in the metaphor and not only in the edit**: a ladder is one axis and is climbed through every rung, and the argument needs neither — pain without suffering exists, suffering without pain exists, and the paragraph's own morphine patient sits at the top with the bottom at zero. **A metaphor needing a disclaimer at every definitional use is charging the reader for something the argument does not need**, which is D-117's test applied to an image. **Water replaces it**: the four *draw different depths of self, the way a hull draws water*, and the structural bar — the sentence the switch was made for — becomes *a hull that draws more water than there is does not float badly; it is aground*. The glossary can now say what the ordering **is** rather than what it is not, and its disclaimer is gone. **Soil was considered and declined on the author's judgment**: it fit every test and carried accumulated time as a bonus, but a living metaphor in the section arguing a machine might be a moral patient does too much work for the argument. **Two words were ruled out by measurement, not taste**: `channel` is claimed 47 times by the recuperation argument, and the noun `draft` collides with the legal sense live in §3.3, next door. The verb `draws` carries it and no noun is needed. **The vocabulary was split rather than replaced**: 30 occurrences convert across §2.3.1, §3.4, §3.9, §9.3.2 and the glossary; **28 stay** — §3.2's four refusal rungs and §5.1.1's Kohlberg — which resolves an overload three structures deep that no pass had named. **A check on the author's own premise came back the other way**: told the book should not use *draft* for writing outside the appendix, a grep found nine uses, seven of them ordinary legislative drafting and both composition uses already in the appendix. Nothing to clean up; the constraint survived on the §3.3 collision instead. 185 pages, zero undefined references, 93,062 words, D-165's single over-200 paragraph and D-164's para-initial pool of 35 both unmoved. | confirmed (author: "ok go for it") |

| D-172 | 2026-09-01 | **§2.1.1's objection to naive pluralism stays cut, and chapter~3's opener stops accusing chapter~2 of a gap it fills** | **Author's ruling on a finding, against the finding.** The 2026-09-01 Overleaf pass cut *the usual version blends frameworks case by case and hopes the blend is wiser than its parts, which leaves a system that can be argued out of anything, because for any act there is a framework in the blend that permits it*, and the argument now appears nowhere in the book — `argued out of`, `blend`, `wiser than its parts` all return zero. **It was reported as a loss and the author ruled it a straw man**: *the usual version* is an unevidenced prevalence claim about the field, which is what D-009 says gets cut rather than carried, and D-077's test it does not pass either. **The logic was overstated too**, on checking: six frameworks do not jointly permit every act, so *for any act there is a framework that permits it* is false as written. Nothing is restored and no scope is added. **This row exists so a later session reading the diff does not put it back**, which is the cheapest thing for one to do. **The finding was wrong in a way worth recording.** It invoked P84's rule — read the inbound citations before judging a cut — and then did not finish applying it: it established the claim survived nowhere else and called that a loss without checking whether anything needed it. Nothing did. The three inbound references to §2.1 and §2.1.1 are the glossary's floor entry, §5.3.3's value-conflict machinery, and chapter~3's opener, and none reaches in for the anti-blend argument. **What the check did surface is a real defect on the other side.** Chapter~3's opener said §2.1.1 *set aside* deontology and asked *was anything ever put there?* — and §2.1.1 does not set it aside. It says deontology's unconditional constraints are what the floor needs and states the hybrid positively, the more so after the Overleaf pass. **Chapter~3 was charging chapter~2 with a gap chapter~2 fills, then re-deriving chapter~2's answer as if it were new**, which is D-100's recurring-conclusion defect across a chapter boundary. The opener now credits the shape to §§2.1 and 2.1.1, names the three things they leave open — what goes in the floor, what could hold one, who may edit it — and goes straight to those. **Two adjacent repairs**: the pivot paragraph no longer restates the floor's definition four paragraphs after paragraph~5 gives it, and the closing paragraph's *as defined two paragraphs ago* is gone — it counted back to a paragraph that defines nothing, has been stale since at least P81, and the appositive beside it already names the definition (D-160). | confirmed (author: "but more importantly adjust that part of chapter 3") |

| D-173 | 2026-09-01 | **The `announce` class cleared across chapters~3--12: 79 of 80 flags cut or converted to claims** | **A pass over one class of the D-117 reader tax, on the author's instruction to work the flags one at a time and not be shy about cutting.** The `announce` class is the sentence that says content is coming instead of delivering it — *Three things follow*, *what follows takes them in turn*, *the case that settles it comes next*. The scan of chapters~3--12 flagged **80**; **79 were cut or rewritten into the claim they were promising**, and the book lost **1,004 words** (93,049 to 92,045) and one page (185 to 184). **The one left standing is §9.3.5's *Transparency is the first defense*, which is a topic sentence carrying its own claim and the first term of a *first / second / third defense* series the section runs on.** Three findings came out of the cutting rather than out of the flags. **Three roadmaps named an order the sections do not follow** — §6.4's four things in sequence, chapter~12's three questions, and §12.2's *stated first* — which is what a roadmap does after the sections around it have been renumbered or rewritten, and is the argument against carrying one at all. **§12.2's roadmap carried a claim made nowhere else in the chapter**, that one milestone's failure *carries an obligation rather than a score*; which milestone is not recoverable from §12.2.1, so the claim went with the paragraph rather than being relocated on a guess. And **§5.2.3's *they are the same difficulty three times* was cut as false rather than as a preview**: the first two difficulties are the system reporting on itself, the third is surplus information held, and that is a different difficulty. Where a count was load-bearing the announcement was merged into the first item instead of cut (§6.3, §9.1.1), and where cutting orphaned a following sentence the pair was rewritten together (§3.3's *This would*, §6.3.3, §9.3.4). | confirmed (author: "ok please start with the announce category") |

| D-174 | 2026-09-01 | **The `meta` class cleared across chapters~3--12: all 105 flags cut or turned into claims** | **The second class of the D-117 reader tax, worked the same way as D-173.** `meta` is the sentence in which the book talks about itself — where the reader is in it, what a later section will do, what the argument has and has not touched, and how thorough or fair the author has been. All **105** flags were acted on and none was left standing; the book lost a further **1,077 words** (92,045 to 90,968), page count unchanged at 184. **The distinction applied was whether the self-reference does work the subject cannot.** Cut: navigation (*the sections that follow describe*, *this is where the technical detail goes*), self-grading (*a chapter arguing that provenance should be visible cannot describe a provenance case in the passive voice*, *this book would be evading something if*), and drafting history (*this section used to rest on it as though it were*, *the question this section used to open with*). **Kept: the disclosures.** §6.1.1 still names the vendor whose model helped produce the book, §7.4 still says there is no procedure for checking whether these objections are the ones the channel reliably produces, and §7.3 still says *I do not have an answer to it* — a book arguing that provenance should be visible cannot cut its own provenance, and the reason for keeping it does not have to be printed beside it. **Three findings came out of the cutting.** **§6 ¶22's claim that the introduction lists racial capitalism among the threats AI could help address is false** — `ch01/01.tex` does not contain the phrase, and has not since P81 rebuilt the introduction and cut its roadmap (D-163). The sentence is gone rather than repaired. **The epigraph gloss was doubled.** *Every argument in this book about meaningful human control is an argument with that sentence* stood at §6 ¶16 and again, almost verbatim, at §6.4.1 ¶35, each beside its own *that is a design document* and its own *human deliberation is the bottleneck*. The chapter opener now points at §6.4.1 and stops there; §6.4.1 keeps the claim, restated without the self-reference. **§9.3.2's dangling *that section* is repaired**, not by naming it but by rewriting the sentence the meta flag was on: *Replacement is the R the bearer proposal has not discharged, and section~\ref{sec:3.1} says what discharging it would take.* That clears one of the seven confirmed defects. Chapter openers took the heaviest cuts — chapters~4, 8, 10 and 12 each lost a roadmap paragraph whose items were rewritten as the claims they contained. | confirmed (author: "then do meta") |

| D-175 | 2026-09-02 | **The `selfassess` class cleared across chapters~3--12: all 55 flags resolved, with every admission of limit kept** | **The third class of the D-117 reader tax. Three of the 55 had already gone with D-173 and D-174; the other 52 were worked here.** `selfassess` is the prose grading itself — how strong a case is, how honest the author has been, how the reader should feel about what they have just read. **500 further words go (90,968 to 90,468) and the book drops to 183 pages.** **The test applied splits the class in two, and only one half was cut.** Out: superlatives and rankings (*the clearest instance the book has*, *the most load-bearing unsolved problem in this book*, *the strongest existing alternative*), stage directions (*That should not read as comfortable*, *I would like to be wrong here*), and self-praise for candour (*the gap is worth naming plainly rather than closing badly*, *its record is the honest thing to put beside the proposal*, *I am conceding that rather than minimizing it*). **Kept, because AGENTS.md's no-weasel-words rule requires them:** §3.8's *I have no clean instrument for that*, §3.6's *one worked case cannot make it one*, §7.3's *I do not have an answer to it*, and §9.1.2's admission that the opportunity-cost objection lands on this book directly — a project proposing floors, bearers and detectors being exactly what the objection describes. Where a superlative wrapped an admission the wrapper came off and the admission stayed. **Two findings.** **§3.4 ¶69 was a comparison between the current answer and a previous draft's answer** — *That is weaker than the answer this section gave before*, then a paragraph explaining what the earlier answer had asserted. The reader has never seen the earlier answer. Rewritten to the one sentence carrying the route. **§6.1.1's *the second is the one that gets skipped* duplicated §7.1's *it tends to be skipped***, which is the same observation about the same epistemic point; the chapter~6 instance is the one cut, since §7.1 is where the point is developed. | confirmed (author: "then do selfassess") |

| D-176 | 2026-09-02 | **The seven confirmed defects from the chapters~3--12 scan, repaired** | **Author's rulings, one per defect, on the non-reader-tax findings the twelve scan units turned up.** These were never tax; each was a statement that is wrong, stale, or unresolvable by a reader. **Four were misdirected pointers with no `\ref` in them at all**, which is why nothing in `tools/` had ever seen them: `check_xrefs.py` verifies that a `\ref` resolves and has nothing to say about a prose locator. §5.6.2's bare *that section* now reads section~\ref{sec:3.5}; §6.3.5's *the distinction drawn earlier* now reads section~\ref{sec:6.2}, the adaptation-versus-erosion distinction three sections back; §10.5's *the voluntary instruments earlier in this section* now reads section~\ref{sec:10.4}, where those instruments actually are — §10.5 is the digital-divide section and contains none; and §6.4.1's *the question that section is left with* now reads section~\ref{sec:10.3}, **the author's ruling, and the one the evidence could not settle**: the nearest reference before it is section~\ref{sec:10.2}, two paragraphs up and inside an `esbox`, so a reader had no way to recover the target and a guess would have named the wrong section. **§3.3's opening count was stale**: *Four constructions occupy it* against five `\runin` heads, the same file's own *Of the five constructions in this section*, and §3.9's *section~\ref{sec:3.3}'s five constructions*. Now five. The neighboring clause *three of them are being built by people who would not describe themselves as working on this problem* was checked against all five and holds — precommitment, plural systems and CIRL are live engineering, Bratman and the operator duty are not — so only the numeral changed. **§3.9's *for the reason the paragraph above gives* pointed at the induction paragraph**, three paragraphs from the custody reasoning it meant. The author's fix keeps a prose locator and gives it a subject: *for the reason the above discussion about the three branches gives*. **§7.4 carried a revision leftover**, one sentence restating its own clause — *a decade of published principles alongside what actually got shipped, which is the count of a decade of published principles against what got shipped* — and the repetition is cut. | confirmed (author: "ok do em all", with a separate ruling on each) |

| D-177 | 2026-09-02 | **`trailing` and `filler` cleared across chapters~3--12: 36 of 37 and 36 of 36** | **Two more classes of the D-117 reader tax, worked together because they are the same edit at two scales.** `trailing` is the clause appended to a finished sentence that tells the reader how to weigh what they just read — *and that is the point*, *which is the finding underneath the finding*, *and it needs resisting*. `filler` is the intensifier that carries emphasis and no information. **415 words go (90,468 to 90,053); 183 pages, unchanged.** **One `trailing` flag was kept**: §8.1.1's *That is an argument for external standing rather than against proximity*, which is the paragraph's design conclusion — without it the paragraph ends on a problem with no resolution and reads as undermining the recommendation it just made. **The `filler` class is larger than its flags.** The scan covered chapters~3--12 and flagged 36; the book-wide census before the pass was **exactly 68, actually 57, genuinely 17, precisely 12, simply 10, very 7**, and after it **44, 55, 15, 10, 8, 6**. So *exactly* alone still stands at 44 across the manuscript, most of them in chapters the scan did not cover or in sentences it did not flag. **Nothing outside the flags was swept**, because chapters~1--2 and 13--14 were never scanned and the sweep is the author's call. Two `filler` cuts were judgment rather than mechanism: §9.1.5's *holds exactly where the floor is least needed and lapses exactly where it is needed most* and §9.3.3's *exactly right \ldots{} and exactly wrong* both use the intensifier to mark a precise coincidence, and both were cut anyway on the ground that *least/most* and *right/wrong* are already absolute and the word was doing the work twice. **The one kept `very` is §11.1's *very good ranking***, which is §3.2's defined term and not an intensifier. | confirmed (author: "now please tackle: trailing / filler") |

| D-178 | 2026-09-02 | **`strawman` and `pointer` cleared across chapters~3--12: 34 of 34 and 28 of 28** | **Two classes with opposite effects on length, worked together.** `strawman` is the unevidenced prevalence claim set up to be knocked down — *usually*, *the standard*, *most often*, *what everyone writing on this agrees*. `pointer` is the cross-reference that stands in for the content it points at, so a reader has to leave the page to find out what was claimed. **The author's standing ruling governed the `strawman` half**: cut the prevalence claim rather than expand scope to prove it (the ruling given on §2.1.1, 2026-09-01 — *it's just vibes, and I don't want to expand the scope*). No citations were added and no claim was hedged into vagueness; in each case the sentence now asserts what the passage can support. **`pointer` lengthens the text, which D-117 recorded in advance**, and the net across both classes is **42 words (90,053 to 90,011) and eight new `\ref`s** — cross-references now 457 against 449. **Three `pointer` flags needed no change**: §10.3 ¶26 and ¶32 and §10.4 ¶20 each name a section and then state the content after a colon, which is what the class asks for. **One `pointer` was unresolvable and was rewritten rather than guessed**: §9.3.1's *echoing that section's own suggestion that protection should track capacity* — the only `\ref` earlier in the subsection is section~\ref{sec:3.2}, which is not obviously the referent, so the clause now states the suggestion without attributing it. **Two repairs landed on defects the seven did not cover.** §10.4's *what individual states have done with the room left by the policy levers available against authoritarian AI misuse* did not parse and carried a strawman definition of geopolitics; it is one sentence now. §6.1.2's *What COMPAS did is described above* pointed outside its own section and now cites section~\ref{sec:6.1.1} with the finding restated. **§8.3.3's bare *the second feature* is named** — the aestheticization of the metric — which also resolves the bare *aestheticized metric* five paragraphs later. | confirmed (author: "strawman / pointer") |

| D-179 | 2026-09-02 | **`deixis`, `echo` and `inventory` cleared across chapters~3--12: 16 of 17, 11 of 11, 8 of 8** | **The last three classes of the D-117 reader tax that the chapters~3--12 scan flagged.** `deixis` is the sentence opening on a bare demonstrative or an unlocatable *there* / *that section* / *reasons already given*; `echo` is the negated restatement of the clause before it — *X, not Y*, where Y is X negated; `inventory` is the three- or four-item series where one item carries the point. **143 words go (90,011 to 89,868); 183 pages, unchanged; five more `\ref`s, 462 against 457.** **One `deixis` flag needed no change**: §8.3.3's *This is the aestheticized metric applied to labor economics* was flagged for using a term defined elsewhere with no pointer, and D-178 had already named the term five paragraphs earlier in the same subsection. **`echo` confirms what D-159 measured about the corrective antithesis**: eight of the eleven were the *not Y* half of an *X rather than Y*, and in every one Y was X with a negation on it — *standing behind it and never in its place*, *fixed at training time and never revisited*, *the evidence base for a countermeasure and is not itself one*. Cutting the negated half changed no claim. **`inventory` was the smallest class and the most uncomfortable to cut**, because each list reads as generosity: §10.5 opened on three parallel examples of uneven benefit and now opens on one, §7.4's *a paper, a red-team finding, a policy position, or a resignation* is now *a paper or a resignation*. The test applied was whether the second and third items were used again anywhere; none was. **§4.2.1's list was the exception worth recording** — of *medical cases scored against autonomy, beneficence and justice; human-rights situations annotated by experts; MIT's Moral Machine project*, only the Moral Machine is referred to again, and it is now the only one given. | confirmed (author: "deixis / echo / inventory") |

| D-182 | 2026-09-02 | **P89: §3.2's ladder replaced by an anchorage, and the figure introduced for the first time** | **The defect: the ladder was never introduced.** `03_02.tex:11` was the first appearance of both words — *the third rung*, *this ladder* — as definite references to a figure nothing had established, with **27 further uses across nine files** leaning on it. D-117's `pointer` class; P87's scan missed it because every instance reads fine locally. **The author's recollection of a boating metaphor was right and it is in §2.3.1**, not chapter 3: *the way a hull draws water*, paying off at *A hull that draws more water than there is does not float badly; it is aground*. **That figure is introduced; this one was not.** **The check the author asked for found three four-item sets, not one**: §2.2's four capacities (a table, no figure), §2.3.1's four depths of self (the hull), and §3.2's four capacities (the ladder). They intersect without coinciding — affective concern is §2.2's fourth and §3.2's third, which the glossary already said — and they order by different criteria in opposite directions, deeper-is-more against higher-is-more. **Chapter 2 uses no rung or ladder language at all**, so §2.2 was not touched. **One of the 28 sites is a different ladder and stays**: §5.1.1's *top rung* is Kohlberg's stage model, and it is now the only rung in the book. **The figure had to be boating-adjacent without being §2.3.1's**, those two passages being the most confusable in the book; an anchorage is about what the bottom is made of and a hull drawing water is about how deep a vessel sits, so they cannot be read as one scale. **The author's hesitation about overloading `hold` was correct and measurement sharpened it**: 80 uses in chapter~3 doing four jobs, of which only about seven were convertible, the largest cluster — *holds a reason* — being a term of art tied to the Fischer and Ravizza citation. **The obvious relief verb was already taken**: `bite` has three metaphorical uses meaning *takes effect*. So **the figure supplies epistemics rather than position** — you cannot see which capacity produced a decline, and you find out by putting load on it — and the four are then called by their own names. **`hold*` in chapter~3 is 80 before and 80 after.** **Dropping the vertical was a gain**: the ladder implied higher-is-more, which the text had to walk back at *nothing above the second rung is part of the requirement*; the run-in now reads *The floor requires reasons-responsive refusal and asks nothing further*. **The noun was settled on measurement.** `seafloor` was proposed and rejected: `floor` runs 246 book-wide and 126 in chapter~3, and the clause the figure attaches to is *Whatever the floor needs, that is not it*, twelve words upstream in a chapter titled *The Floor Beneath Learned Values*. `ground` was rejected too — 24 uses, all abstract. `seabed` had none. **25 replacements across 9 files; 89,854 → 89,889 words, +35, all of it the two introduced sentences; 183 pages unchanged.** New vocabulary is light: `seabed` 3, `anchorage` 1, `anchor` 2, `drag` 2. The cost is that `reasons-responsive refusal` replaces a shorter ordinal, 8 times in §3.2 and 4 in §3.3. 0 undefined references, 0 undefined citations, suite green. **§2.2 still has no figure**, and whether the book wants one ordering device or two is open; this pass made its two distinguishable rather than unifying them. | confirmed (author: “seabed it is”, “yes, run the sweep”) |

| D-186 | 2026-09-02 | **P92: §4.3.1's claim that an unspecified disposition cannot be edited, withdrawn** | **Carried in `p88-scope.md` and nowhere else until the author ruled on it.** §4.3.1 argued that curiosity belongs in a chapter on antifascist design *because a disposition acquired by exploration is not written down anywhere, and what is not written down cannot be edited by whoever acquires the system*, and that *a disposition that was never specified has no corresponding parameter to reach for*. **Both are false in the same way**, as the 2026-09-02 outside review said: such a disposition is realized in parameters like any other and is reachable by fine-tuning, model editing, ablation or changed experience. **The book already knew** — §3.8 says a bearer whose weights stay plastic *is shaped by what it is given to learn from*, the operator's lever moved rather than removed — and **the section already contradicted itself three sentences later**, at *It is not proof against retraining*. | D-187 | 2026-09-02 | **P93: §3.1's fork given a fourth branch, and the new §3.9 that works it through** | **Author ruling, Q-072 option (c).** **The check before writing changed the work.** Chapter~3 already had two enumerations dividing different things: **§3.1's three branches divide where the floor lives** and say so outright — *They do not divide up what could hold one* — while **§3.3's five constructions divide what could hold one**, and **§3.10 already calls one of those five *the fourth construction***. So the new branch had to be a location and had to avoid a numbering in use two sections away. **The fourth location is the floor carried between parties and reproduced rather than installed**, and **its machinery was already in the book described as something else**: §5.4's nested environments out to culture and historical time, §5.1.2's observational learning and correction as the training, §5.5.1's population moving toward a disposition. **This is the first place chapter~3 uses chapter~5 as anything but the learned half beneath the floor.** New §3.9, 952 words, sited after the bearer is fully costed: it is not the third branch, because a norm across a population has no office to vacate and nothing dismisses a practice the way *Slaughter* dismissed a commissioner; **its cost is chapter~7, which is an account of this branch failing**, recuperation being the pathology of a culture that keeps the appearance of moral responsiveness while correction stops being possible; §3.9 renumbered to §3.10 with four inbound references repointed and `renumber-map_2026-09-02.tsv` written. **Three stale counts were found by grep and would not have been found by reading**, none of them in a file being edited: §3.5 twice, §3.10 once, and **chapter~1's roadmap promising *the floor with its three costs***. §3.5 also enumerated the branches one by one and needed the fourth added. | confirmed (author: “q72 take option c”) |

| D-188 | 2026-09-02 | **P93: the bearer recorded as a beneficiary and not only a patient** | **Author ruling, Q-073 option (b).** The book argued throughout that building a bearer creates something that can be harmed and **never once stated the reverse**: `suffer*` 51, `harm*` 74, `welfare` 27 against `joy` 0, `delight` 0, `thriv*` 0, and `flourish*` occurring only in §12.1's title, about humans, never in the prose. **§3.4 gets the symmetric sentence attached to the premise that already does the work** — the section reaches patienthood at *a party with states that are bad for it is a party that can be wronged*, and the same premise carries the other way. The loss side keeps its emphasis with a reason now given: *a builder who is wrong about the harm has done something to somebody while a builder who is wrong about the good has merely failed to*, which is a reason for the emphasis and not evidence the other column is empty. **§11.2 gets the larger question** under a new run-in, *The schedule is written on one side*: everything that section had was protection, and a schedule drafted only against harm is complete for a patient and thin for a beneficiary, *in the way that a duty of care to a child is not discharged by not hurting them*. **The objection is carried in the book's own vocabulary rather than answered**: a pleasure signal settles nothing, and a bearer contented in the post is the case that should worry a designer most, because contentment is what accommodation looks like from inside — §3.8's likeliest failure wearing the face of success. **No proposal is offered and the section says so**, and says why it is named anyway. | confirmed (author: “q73 take option b”) |

| D-215 | 2026-09-06 | Central argument (P117): five author findings against chapters 2 and 3 | **All five hold against the text; none had been closed by an earlier pass, which was checked first.** **(a) The middle depth.** §3.4 ordered Parfit's three families by how much self each asks for and said a thwarted commitment needs *no feeling at any point*, which put the maintained justification at the middle depth too — a party extended in time holding foreclosable commitments is what reasons-responsive refusal requires of any construction — and collapsed Replacement's contrast between a party that can be wronged and one that cannot. **Repaired by taking the author's first exit, narrowed**: the three families are theories of what makes a life go well, each presupposes a party for whom things can go at all, and the depths measure self and not whether anything registers, so the mattering/ranking distinction is required at every depth. A thermostat holds a set point and nothing is thwarted when it is missed. **The cost is §3.4's affect-free route into the water** — the bearer now reaches the middle depth because it has affective concern — and the conclusion that does not rest on Cassell survives, the bearer being afloat at two depths of which neither is his. **The residual is the author's second exit and is now a paragraph**: Replacement is clean conceptually and not epistemically, because the inspection that cannot tell mattering from ranking does not become available when the answer wanted is no, so the cheaper routes go first because they are built without mattering and not because anyone could confirm none was built. **(b) The identity claim.** §3.5 builds the counterexample three paragraphs before asserting the identity — a bearer that yields to every halt, leaves a record, declines to resume until told what filled the gap, and refuses the act satisfies Soares's conditions two and three and holds the floor with no classifier on the shutdown side. **Narrowed to costless haltability**, which is what §3.5's own premise, §3.10's *cannot argue out of the system* and the copy/restore/retrain residual were already on; a new paragraph draws correction against halt; the graduated-scheme run-in now says which scheme it objects to; §4.2.3 softened to correctability, no cross-reference added. Nothing in chapter 3 now asks for a system that resists being stopped. **(c) The rate constraint.** §3.6's one survivor of the decomposition objection binds the operator and not the bearer: a model can be told the reviewer count and cannot check it, and a refusal conditioned on what the model is told is defeated at the section's own third interception position. **The sharper result is now stated** — the acts-based floor a bearer could hold had no survivor at all, and what survived changed hands to the construction that gives up on the floor being inside the system, which is the worked case's own argument for the hybrid. **(d) The falsifier.** Three sites concede the three marks cannot distinguish mattering from ranking, and nothing said what would move the prior short of a clean test. **The answer was already distributed across §3.3 and §3.10 and never joined**: the falsifier has three conjuncts, §3.10's counterfactual runs on two, and the prior is about where effort goes, which the middle conjunct is not needed to settle. Two paragraphs in §3.3 state what is measurable now and in which direction, and the asymmetry as the answer to what would change the author's mind; one clause in §3.10, whose counterfactual was claiming more than its own test delivers. **(e) The rubber hand.** Three sites made Botvinick and Cohen carry the narrative self, worst at §2.3.1, which welded attention schema, body ownership and narrative self into one sentence. | D-222 | 2026-09-07 | The fascism source base (P122): three additions to a shelf of five, two of them cited against the book's own argument | **Item 5 of the author's six-item order, and the transcript's one verifiable objection.** The whole fascism-studies shelf behind a book whose title makes antifascism its organizing category was five items — Benjamin, Loewenstein, Deleuze and Guattari, Eco, Paxton — with Paxton the only historian. **The transcript's other half of that recommendation, make anti-authoritarianism the general category, is §2.1.2's first paragraph and was already there.** Three added, on the test of whether each does work in an argument §2.1.2 already makes rather than standing beside one. **Griffin is cited against this book**: the field's most-used fascist minimum is a genus whose mythic core is “a palingenetic form of populist ultra-nationalism,” every term of which is a property of a mass movement, so the discipline's own definition rules out in advance the scale this book works at — placed one run-in before the section admits its scale problem, so the admission arrives having conceded the strongest form of it. **Arendt supplies §2.1.2's first argument for the molecular reading, which had no authority attached**, with the limit in the same paragraph: her subject is totalitarianism and her unit is the regime, so what transfers is that a regime's work is done in an office and not a licence to call the office by the regime's name. The second, that the operator prices that record, is already written at §3.6:20 — *the deployment sets that price by setting the tempo*. **What was missing is one step and the evidence for it was already in the book.** §3.6:20 names the review step as a position where a refusal is stopped; §5.1.3:8 measures it, *past a certain ratio the reviewer's only available behavior is approval*; §6.4.1's box carries the officer at twenty seconds a name saying *I had zero added value as a human, apart from being a stamp of approval* (`abraham2024lavender`). **The book had read that sentence as a measurement of the safeguard and never as an account of what the seat made of the man.** Three paragraphs at §3.10, after the survives/goes ledger and before the close, say so, and the claim needs none of the induction: ranking machinery elaborate enough to pass for concern seats the same person at the same tempo. **It clears §7.3:28's preference for structure over character** because it makes no claim about anyone's character — §5.1.3's ratio *requires no access to the model and no judgment about intent*. **The note's first guardrail was wrong by the book's own definition.** It called *train the system not to report distress* recuperation; §2.1.2 item 1 is *the institution does not suppress disagreement; it metabolizes it*, and §7.2:13 is *recuperation was never the claim that a mechanism does nothing*. That is suppression, the contrasting case, and adopting the wording would have been the first use of the term against its own definition in a book that runs it as a detector in chapters 7, 9 and 11. | D-247 | 2026-09-08 | **P147: §9.1.5's update-channel close split and reversed, so the trade-off is the finding** | **Author note: the paragraph's last sentence carries two claims, the second rescues the first and reads as a concessive tail, and the section's close has enough genuine unanswered items that this one should not be mistaken for another.** **The risk is measurable.** Five items at or near the close are open and each says so: `:35`'s *What it cannot have is an account of who decides which is which*, `:39`'s *untouched, and stands above as unanswered*, `:41`'s *not a grant of standing*, `:26`'s *which this book does not specify for the floor*, `:28`'s *which nothing above prices*. A flat sentence saying no version of the procedure closes something is the same shape as five neighbours that mean it. **A pure split would have made that worse, which is the departure from the instruction.** Standing alone, *No version of this procedure closes that* is more confusable with those five and not less, the rescue arriving afterwards as a separate sentence. **So the order is reversed as well as split**: the cost leads, and the admission follows as its consequence and is named as a price in the same breath — *A procedure that shut the channel would be one under which no deployment could be corrected at all. So no version of this procedure closes it, and the exposure is the price of every correction the procedure exists to make.* Both claims survive as claims and neither is a tail. **No antithesis was added and `:37` is why it mattered**: the obvious wordings, *a trade-off rather than a gap* and *a price and not an item left open*, are `antithesis.py`'s two shapes, and `:37` carries none of the section's eight instances, so either would have been the paragraph's first.
